# Rust → Lean operations audit

## Scope, provenance and reading rules

Read-only source audit performed 2026-11-12. Only this document is written. No builds, tests, executable help invocations, service operations, network calls, commits or subagents were used by this audit agent. Secret **names**, never secret values, are included.

- Requested Rust reference **R**: `4199fc06e^`, resolved to `b22ed3181df64cf9681e887381ede7ed15fe96aa`.
- Requested current reference **L**: `5383bf27b8f8e664b654339cf88e14c90dba665c` (HEAD at audit start).
- Important baseline limitation: R contains `src/main.rs`, `src/config.rs`, the Rust server/node libraries and Tauri desktop, but **no `src/bin/`**. Companion binaries and the node entrypoint had already been retired. Their replacements under `lean/OrbBin` and `lean/OrbNode` already exist at R. Do not describe their subsequent issues as introduced by the final Rust deletion without earlier evidence.
- Supplemental **C**: `bb3dae11d^`, the parent of companion retirement #1575, for `src/bin/{automation_manager_mcp,desktop_mcp,fido_agent_proxy,palomactl,sandboxed_mcp,workspace_mcp}.rs`, `src/bin/palomactl/models.rs`, and `crates/lean-mesh/src/bin/lean-mesh.rs`.
- Supplemental **N**: `3661fdc0c^`, the parent of node retirement #1588, for `src/bin/sandboxed_node.rs`. R retains the capacity library used by that entrypoint.
- `R:path:line`, `L:path:line`, `C:path:line`, and `N:path:line` refer to those exact snapshots, not line numbers in an absent working-tree Rust file. Reproduce a historical citation with `git show <ref>:<path>` and number lines starting at 1.
- Guidance read: `AGENTS.md:1-27,101-114,375-378,421-433`; both running plans, `docs/plans/core-recovery-2026-11.md` and `docs/plans/managed-providers-leanci-colab.md`.

### Classification

| Code | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| E | Equivalent for the **explicitly stated behavior**, supported by implementation evidence; not an assertion of whole-file parity. |
| D | Different implementation or contract; identified intentional changes are explicitly called out. D alone is not a bug. |
| S | Stub/placeholder implementation: exposes a surface but does not do the stated work. |
| M | Missing old behavior/field/consumer integration, demonstrated by an inspected replacement path. |
| U | Unresolved: discovery evidence, not enough semantic comparison to claim parity or a defect. |

Mixed rows classify individual aspects, not a blanket file. In particular, matching filenames, environment names, command names or JSON keys **never establishes E**. Discovery appendices deliberately use U unless covered by the semantic tables. This is a broad inventory and focused correctness audit, **not a certification that every discovered variable/field is honored**. The coordinator supplied independent second-reader verdicts on OPS-01..04 before final handoff; those corrections are incorporated and recorded below. This audit agent itself launched no subagents.

## Executive findings

Three concrete Rust-node-to-Lean regressions and one **inherited conditional packaging warning** are separated from the running plans' already-owned incidents. All have source-level evidence and a one-PR repair boundary. Runtime impact is conditional on the described configuration; this audit does not claim to have observed a production failure. OPS-01..03 are node-adjacent comparisons using N's retired entrypoint plus R's retained Rust library, **not newly attributed to the final R→L Core cutover**.

| ID | Finding | Classification | Priority / confidence | Ownership |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OPS-01 | A stale `capacity_override.json` overrides a newer node env-file edit; restart comparison assumes the service imports the edited env | D | P2 / high | Worker configuration precedence fix; node-port provenance |
| OPS-02 | Env reload omits side/coordinator capacity and disk floor; override loading omits side capacity/disk floor | M | P2 / high | Worker loader/migration field parity; not a new two-field API/write regression |
| OPS-03 | Node env-file mtime watching disappeared; edits no longer take effect without a signal/restart | M | P2 / high | Worker reload trigger fix; node-port provenance |
| OPS-04 | Python-only unit launcher is incompatible **if** a native broker is installed at its target path | D/U | Boundary warning / high on conditional mismatch; exposure unverified | Inherited packaging boundary; coordinate recovery §5 privd integration, not counted as new cutover defect |

OPS-01/02/03 are distinct precedence, loader field application and trigger failures. They can land separately; if combined, keep all three acceptance cases. None is the recovery plan's provider-fleet reconciler, fake remote-node liveness or compute-rollup incident. Independent review confirmed these three with the qualifications below and downgraded OPS-04 to the packaging warning.

### OPS-01 — newer operator env edits lose to stale UI override forever

**Both-side evidence.** R `src/node/capacity.rs:534-552,570-609` checks modification times: the override wins only when newer than the env file. N `src/bin/sandboxed_node.rs:124-129,149-156` wires startup and live reload to that policy. L `lean/OrbNode/OrbNode/Runner.lean:140-151` first reads env-file capacity and then unconditionally replaces it from `capacity_override.json`. L `Runner.lean:1221-1234` repeats the unconditional override during reload. There is no mtime comparison in either path.

**Source-derived scenario (not executed).** Persist a UI override with `capacity=8`; subsequently edit the configured env file to `SANDBOXED_NODE_CAPACITY=2`. On a reload signal, Lean reselects 8; Rust rereads the file and selects 2 because the env file is newer. For the **restart** comparison, the node service must import the edited file through `EnvironmentFile=` (or otherwise provide the edited process env): N `src/bin/sandboxed_node.rs:106-109` initializes ordinary capacity from process env, not by directly reading the env file. With that condition, Rust startup also retains 2 rather than the stale override. Equal mtimes favor the env side (`R:src/node/capacity.rs:607` uses strict `>`). This defeats an operator reducing concurrency under memory pressure. Override persistence is not itself wrong: preserving a *newer* UI override was intentional in Rust.

**One PR.** Add one shared env/override freshness resolver and use it at startup and reload; preserve newer UI overrides, equal-mtime env preference and missing-file behavior, and report malformed configuration without replacing valid live state. Do not merely delete override persistence. Acceptance cases: env newer, override newer, equal timestamps, missing env, malformed override, process-env import on restart, and active jobs surviving resize. No test was executed here. Confidence high. Independent second reader confirmed the precedence defect with this restart qualification.

### OPS-02 — loader/reload coverage silently lost operational resource fields

**Both-side evidence.** R `src/node/capacity.rs:158-178` defines `capacity`, `side_capacity`, `coordinator_capacity`, `min_free_gb`, `updated_at` in the persistent override; `:259-280` parses all four resource settings from the env file; `:493-507,581-594` applies them, including notifying disk GC when its floor changes. L `Runner.lean:74-88` parses only `SANDBOXED_NODE_CAPACITY`. L `:140-151,1221-1234` reads only `capacity` and `coordinator_capacity` from overrides. Side capacity and disk floor are still real startup settings at L `Runner.lean:110-114,132-136`, not retired concepts; `scripts/bootstrap_baremetal_worker.sh:10,27,42,154-156` still provisions them.

**Important narrowing.** L `Runner.lean:1193-1216` and `Server.lean:660-666` accept/write only two API-update fields, but that restriction is **inherited**: N `src/bin/sandboxed_node.rs:534-556` also updates ordinary/coordinator capacity and persists `side_capacity: None`, `min_free_gb: None`. Do not count two-field API admission or failure to retain those fields in a newly written override as a new regression. The confirmed regression is **reading/applying** fields from legacy/manual valid overrides and env reload, not an expanded API contract that Rust never exposed.

**Source-derived scenario (not executed).** (a) Change `SANDBOXED_NODE_SIDE_CAPACITY`, `SANDBOXED_NODE_COORDINATOR_CAPACITY` or `SANDBOXED_NODE_MIN_FREE_GB` in the env file and deliver SIGHUP: only ordinary capacity is reread. (b) Supply a valid Rust-schema override containing `side_capacity` and `min_free_gb`, newer than the env file: Rust loads those optional fields; Lean ignores them at startup and live reload, using startup env/defaults instead. This does not assert that the old two-field UI endpoint itself wrote such overrides. A lower desired side cap or higher desired free-disk floor therefore does not apply. Startup process-env variables themselves are not entirely missing.

**One PR.** Introduce a typed loader snapshot shared by env parsing, legacy override decoding and live application. Restore the missing read/apply behavior, including disk-floor admission/GC consumers; keep active jobs and shrink debt semantics. Acceptance cases independently cover all env fields, startup/live loading of optional legacy JSON fields, absent optional fields and malformed updates. Expanding the two-field HTTP API or preserving optional fields on every API write would be a **separate retention improvement**, not required evidence for this bug. Confidence high; confirmed with this narrowing by independent second reader. Separate from OPS-01 freshness and OPS-03 polling.

### OPS-03 — changing the node env file no longer reloads it automatically

**Both-side evidence.** R `src/node/capacity.rs:629-679` selects between SIGHUP and an interval, compares env-file mtime, and reloads when it changes. N `src/bin/sandboxed_node.rs:149-157` starts this watcher. L `lean/OrbNode/OrbNode/Server.lean:1899-1955` has the complete server loop: `reloadCapacityFromFiles` is invoked only in the SIGHUP branch (`:1914-1918`). Normal accept timeouts tick the job runtime (`:1923-1929`), not the config loader. Its other callers are not a periodic watcher. Therefore fixing the contents or precedence of reload alone does not restore this behavior.

**Source-derived scenario (not executed).** Start a node without an override, edit ordinary capacity in its configured env file, and wait without sending a signal. Rust eventually resizes its pool; Lean retains the old capacity indefinitely. The failure persists even if OPS-01 and OPS-02 are fixed.

**One PR.** Restore a bounded mtime poll using the same reload function as SIGHUP, without blocking the request mutex on every accept or restarting jobs. Acceptance cases: a changed env file reloads, unchanged files do not repeatedly resize, invalid edits preserve live settings, and SIGHUP remains immediate. Confidence high; independent second reader confirmed OPS-03.

### OPS-04 — inherited conditional native/Python service packaging boundary

**Both-side evidence and chronology.** R `deploy/systemd/orb-privd.service:15` invokes `/usr/bin/python3 /usr/local/sbin/orb-privd`, compatible with R `scripts/orb-privd.py:1-14`. But native privd already existed at R: `lean/OrbBin/lakefile.lean:39-41` declares it and `scripts/build_orb_bins.sh:80-83` stages it. L `lean/OrbBin/OrbBin/PrivdMain.lean:1325-1385` and `scripts/build_orb_bins.sh:139` retain the native artifact, while L `deploy/systemd/orb-privd.service:18` retains the Python launcher. The Python source was **renamed to** `scripts/privd_confinement_model.py`, not simply erased as an implementation: L `:1404-1412,1432-1439` still contains listener setup and a main entrypoint. Native `spawn_user_job` callers are L `lean/OrbNode/OrbNode/Runner.lean:1632,2384` through `PrivdClient.lean`; the earlier CPU-property anchors `:1438-1439` are not the consumer call sites.

**Conditional reproduction / exposure limit.** **If** a native broker is installed at `/usr/local/sbin/orb-privd`, starting the checked-in unit makes Python parse ELF rather than execute it, preventing broker startup. If the path instead contains a compatible Python listener, that specific launcher mismatch does not occur. Neither the audited staging script nor this source review demonstrates installation of the native artifact at that path or what any live host contains. The native/Python coexistence predates the final cutover, so this is **not a new R→L defect and is not included in the three confirmed node regressions**.

**One-PR boundary cleanup, if the native installation is intended.** Execute the installed broker directly (`ExecStart=/usr/local/sbin/orb-privd`), align installation path/artifact identity, and add an executable/interpreter packaging assertion. Direct execution can preserve a properly shebanged legacy Python artifact too. Check intended native and Python/confinement-model roles first; do not remove the Python listener on the assumption it is dead code. Coordinate this boundary check with recovery §5 privd integration. Confidence high in the conditional interpreter mismatch, **deployment exposure unresolved**. Independent second reader requested this downgrade and corrected the chronology/consumer anchors.

## False positives and intentional changes explicitly ruled out

1. **“All Rust bins disappeared in 4199fc06e.”** False for this baseline: `src/bin` was already absent. R→L cannot establish the original companion/node port regressions without C/N. OPS-01/02/03 are Rust→Lean regressions, not asserted to have first appeared after R.
2. **“No Rust `sd-notify` means systemd watchdog is missing.”** False: Core has its own notify/watchdog consumer at L `Control.lean:6471-6473` and surrounding server loop.
3. **“LIBRARY_PATH was removed.”** False from the top-level config diff alone: L `ResourcesAndSystem.lean:1003` reads it.
4. **“Missing generated config filenames proves no MCP.”** False: L launcher uses native flags/env/private temporary overlays. What remains U is non-MCP configuration/profile/hook parity, not the existence of injection.
5. **“Named deploy proves old deployment safety.”** False in the other direction: restart guard is not build/install/health/rollback. Existing recovery owner, not a new OPS ID.
6. **“Boat still only writes a DB row.”** Stale running-plan starting-state description, not HEAD: shared provisioning and guest script now exist. Other providers/async receipt/keys remain owned by the provider mission.
7. **“reconcile must honor SANDBOXED_API_URL to choose live mode.”** Both C and L select that branch only from explicit `--api`; inherited, not a new Lean regression.
8. **“bunker-step clamps very old heartbeats at simulation origin.”** Inherited C mesh `:472-473` and L `MeshMain.lean:261-265`; not newly introduced. It is a simulation CLI, not proof that the live heartbeat enforcer does the same.
9. **“Old orb-node reload name mismatch is new.”** R already shipped the orb-node unit alongside sandboxed-node-only CLI validation; current integration should be reviewed, but not counted as a newly proven R→L defect.
10. **“Matching env/key names means equivalent behavior.”** Explicitly not assumed. All discovery-only entries below remain U; token presence may be a read, a generated child env, a denylist item or a comment, not an honored startup setting.

## Independent review handoff / limits

The coordinator supplied an independent second-reader result before handoff. Verdicts: **OPS-01 confirmed with restart process-env import and equal-mtime qualifications; OPS-02 confirmed for loaders/startup override/live reload only, with two-field API/write behavior inherited; OPS-03 confirmed; OPS-04 downgraded to inherited conditional packaging warning, deployment exposure unverified.** This agent checked the additional anchors directly: R OrbBin lakefile `:39-41`, R build script `:80-83`, N node API `:534-556`, L Python listener `privd_confinement_model.py:1404-1412,1432-1439`, and L node broker callers `Runner.lean:1632,2384`.

Confirm no other active mission owns the exact node fix before opening implementation PRs; coordinate OPS-04 with recovery §5. Recommended acceptance checks are stated above but **not run**. No generated executable, systemd state, live credential, deployed env file or secret store was opened. Second-reader review covered the findings, not every U inventory row.

The appendices below are reproducible static discovery indexes over the pinned trees. They make the breadth auditable, expose unreviewed areas instead of hiding them, and intentionally do not promote lexical overlap to equivalence. Dynamic/computed env names, extensionless config paths, indirect shell expansion and schemas defined in dependencies may escape lexical extraction. Full HTTP/MCP tool semantics, every desktop RPC command, and all persistence schema invariants require their own route/state audits; here they are considered as CLI/config consumers where encountered, not certified end to end.

## Appendix B — environment and generated-environment discovery index

Names and source anchors only; **no values**. Both process readers and producers/forwarders/allowlists/denylists are relevant. This conservative lexical index also retains uppercase underscore literals that may be non-env constants (for example API enum labels); these are U, not alleged supported environment switches. It excludes dedicated test/spec files and Rust `#[cfg(test)]` tails. Array-based and dynamically constructed names require follow-up. Short direct env reads and shell expansions are included. Empty side means no literal in this scanned corpus, **not** proof of no equivalent. Supplementary old binary env names are indexed separately in Appendix C.

## Appendix E — disk/config/materialization field discovery

Selected configuration/launcher/store consumers, not an assertion that all their JSON fields are disk fields. Includes object-entry literals, field accesses and config/path literals with **first occurrence per file** for readability. Some are RPC/response fields or denylisted filenames rather than materialized fields. Structural destinations/semantics are distinguished in the reviewed table; all remaining entries U. Values, payload content and credentials are omitted. Dynamic paths and nested interpolation need manual tracing.

- Tracked `scripts/` files: R 113, L 116. Tracked `deploy/` files: R 23, L 23.

## Appendix G — script-owned parser declarations and subcommands

Static parser declarations (no execution). Python `add_argument` rows distinguish actual parser inputs from the child-command option tokens in Appendix F. Parser choices are listed only when literal; their order does not imply semantics. Shell case alternatives are restricted to a `case` on the first positional argument and remain U because branches may dispatch external commands. Removed or changed Rust-era parser declarations are retained. Scripts with only positional `sys.argv`/shell references or a custom parser remain covered by the full-file ledger and need manual interpretation; no declaration found is not proof of no CLI.
